# The Front-End Enablement Implementation Plan

> **WRITTEN 2026-09-27, OVERNIGHT, AT RICH'S INSTRUCTION; ITS SITTING 1 (TASK 1, THE MEASUREMENTS, WHICH BUILD NOTHING) RAN THE SAME NIGHT, BEFORE HIS REVIEW. REVIEWED AND APPROVED BY RICH LATER ON 2026-09-27: the plan as written (*"all seems good"*), all thirty-one decisions as recommended, the twelve-sitting split, binary files, and all four spec actions as recommended — APPLIED to the spec the same day (`1d1afd7`).** Rich asked for
> *"one plan which encompasses all of these"* and, going to bed, for it to be written *"with your recommendations for any
> items that come up"*, to be reviewed and adjusted in the morning. **He reviewed it and changed nothing** (*Decided by
> Rich*, its last bullet), so every decision below his line is now decided, and no task waits on a spec action. Every fact in *Read this first* was read from the code at `e1712d8` on 2026-09-27; Task 1 re-measured
> each one into `spikes/frontend-baseline/`, and the `[M<n>]` block at the head of a task is what it corrected. *What executing this plan found* is where every sitting's record goes.

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking. **Commit on `main`; no branch, no worktree, no push** — ORIENTATION §6 rule 9, which both of those skills will push you against.

**Goal:** The faculty front-end — a separate project, served at `app.manifest.internal` — can be built against Manifest. A person signs in **on that origin**. The front-end's own agent is issued **a model key charged to that person**, bounded by a per-session cap and a monthly budget, and routed by the project's data classification. The agent **reads what a running app printed**, in sandbox and staging, and lists an environment's instances. It commits **images, PDFs and fonts** beside text. A project has **a name people read**. A person adds a colleague **by CWL login name or email**. An owner can **switch an app off and bring it back** (archive and restore), and can **delete one that never launched**. It is proved by `make demo-frontend`: a front-end's server, on the `app` origin, drives the whole of it for a signed-in instructor, and a person clicks it.

**Architecture:** Eight additions to the API one front-end needs, on the patterns the authoring API left: `defineRoute` with captured `examples`, the reference's completeness gate, the authorization matrix, the mock answering from the document, the console's coverage gate, and a guide whose code is run. **Recent output** is a bounded read through the `Driver`'s `logs`, now bounded in bytes as well as lines. It shares ONE reader and ONE redactor with `Incident.log_tail`, so §14's *"redacted at read with the rules that redact `Incident.log_tail`"* is one code path, not two. **The second origin** is a configured list. The CSRF check, the SAML client a sign-in and a step-up use, and the sign-out's destination each resolve the origin a request ARRIVED on (the sign-in's `returnTo` stays a PATH, so it never leaves that origin), and the control plane's one SP entity carries one assertion-consumer URL per origin. **Agent sessions** are a new table and a LiteLLM user per PERSON. A key is minted per session with a `duration`, a `max_budget` and the models D17 allows, is identified by its alias, and is ended with the session, the token that asked for it, or the project. **Archive** puts the project in a state that `assertCapability` refuses for every change. It then tears down what runs — idempotently, re-run at boot — and points each hostname at a platform page. **Delete** archives, destroys what archive kept, and leaves a tombstone, because the audit trail is append-only by grant and references the row. **Binary files** are an `encoding` on a write and a read, confined to a list of media types recognised by their bytes, and scanned for secrets by their printable runs.

**Tech Stack:** TypeScript 5.9.3 on Node **24.12.0** (host), Fastify **5.12.3**, Drizzle over Postgres 16, `zod/v4` for the API (the spec module is still `zod` 3.25.76's v3 API), Vitest 2.1, `git` **2.50.1** (Apple, host). React 19.3.0 + Vite 8.3.0 in `packages/console`; `@manifest/contract` generated by `openapi-typescript` 7.13.0 through `openapi-fetch` 0.17.0; Ajv 8.20.0 in `@manifest/mock`. LiteLLM **1.98.0** (§21's inventory), SimpleSAMLphp as `infra/idp/` builds it, Caddy as `infra/images.lock` pins it. **No new external package**: media types are recognised by their magic bytes by hand (Decision 9), because a sniffing library is a dependency and a network fetch for ten byte patterns.

**Spec:** [`../specs/2026-08-29-manifest-platform-design.md`](../specs/2026-08-29-manifest-platform-design.md). Read these sections: **§14** in full (its second bullet as amended on 2026-09-26 — the bounded read — and *Redaction at capture*), **§10** (every paragraph, and the key table), **§9**'s first paragraph and *Local behaviour*, **§20** *Manifest's own front door* and *Credential classes (D24)*, **§21** *The front-end in the local topology*, **§22** (the reference console, the journey, D23's nine principles), **§23** *The three platform zones* (the reserved labels) and D26, **§11** *Lifetime policies*, **§13** *Roles*, **§6**'s `User`, `Project`, `Instance`, `AgentSession` and `DelegatedToken`, **§17**'s Phase 2 and Phase 3 rows, and D2, D8, D14, D17, D22, D23, D24 and D26 in §4. **The four spec actions below change §6, §9, §10, §11, §13, §15, §17, §20, §21, §22 (D23.9), D2, D8 and D24 — read them before the sitting that builds each.**

**Roadmap:** the section *The front-end enablement plan* in [`2026-08-29-plan-roadmap.md`](./2026-08-29-plan-roadmap.md) — **it replaces *A running app's recent output — Spec action 1's plan***, which this plan's Tasks 2 and 3 build — and its rule that anything widening who can change a spec goes after the gate that inspects spec changes (P6b's, proved). **This plan is the first to hand an agent outside Manifest a MODEL credential**, and the first to let an owner take a running app DOWN. Write every control as though a prompt-injected agent will arrive at it, because D14 says one will.

**Predecessor:** [`2026-09-25-authoring-api.md`](./2026-09-25-authoring-api.md), executed 2026-09-27. **Its *What this plan does not build* is an input list**: the recent-output read (Spec action 1, applied to §14 on 2026-09-26) is Tasks 2–3; *no operation lists an environment's instances* (its sitting 10's F13) is Task 3; its sitting 10's F1 (the knowledge pack's missing `express.urlencoded`), F4 (the seed read as *pushed with git*) and the whole-branch review's six minors (F7–F12) are Task 5; binary files, deleting a project and the audience's change are named there — the first two are built here, the third is not.

**Successor:** the faculty front-end's LAUNCH PATH (FE-6, FE-25) — then the vulnerability database in the console (Rich, 2026-09-27, in sitting 7's first message: *"Launch path first"*; it had been the vulnerability database since 2026-09-24). **Then the faculty front-end itself**, designed screen by screen against this plan's API (Rich, 2026-09-27: *"think through, screen by screen, and even step-by-step what the faculty experience is like"*), in its own project (§5).

## How this plan is to be executed — sittings, one per session

**One sitting per session, with a check-in at each boundary.** This pattern has carried every plan since P4a. It means a session limit never lands in the middle of a task. This plan commits after every task: a stop *between* tasks is recoverable, a stop *inside* one is not.

> **TWELVE SITTINGS — APPROVED BY RICH ON 2026-09-27** (*"12 sittings is fine"*). 1 the measurements; 2 recent output, end to end; 3 binary
> files; 4 what the authoring API hands over; 5 a project's name and people by CWL login; 6 the `app` origin; 7 agent
> sessions; 8 archive and restore; 9 delete; 10 the console and the mock; 11 the guides; 12 the acceptance. **The order
> is chosen so the spec actions are needed as LATE as possible**: sittings 2–4 build nothing a spec action changes (§14's
> read is already applied; binary files and the inheritance change no spec text), so Rich can decide Spec actions 4, 2, 1
> and 3 before sittings 5, 6, 7 and 8 respectively. *(He decided all four at once, on 2026-09-27, before sitting 2 — so
> the order no longer waits on him; it stands because nothing else argues for changing it.)* **The heavy ones are 7 (a new credential, two tasks) and 8 (a teardown
> across every module)**: if 7 runs long, stop after Task 9 and sweep, and Task 10 opens sitting 8 ahead of Task 11.
> *Offered, and not chosen:* **eleven** — Tasks 11 and 12 in one sitting, which puts the plan's two destructive operations
> and their controls in one session's context; **thirteen** — Task 13 split into the console and the mock, which buys
> nothing a sitting boundary needs.

*The **Status** column records what a sitting made true, never how many findings it produced. That number lives once, in the roadmap's defect-rate table (Rich, 2026-09-20).*

**EVERY SITTING ENDS THE SAME WAY, and none of these four steps is optional:**

1. **The four gates**, from the repository root: `pnpm test` (twice, alone), `pnpm lint`, `pnpm typecheck` and `pnpm format:check`. Add **`pnpm test:docker`** whenever the table above says it is owed (957 s on its last run, 2026-09-26). **Budget for it rather than being surprised by it, run it in the background, and restart the control plane afterwards.** **The test budget is LEAN** (ORIENTATION §8 *Decided*): no open run on an unchanged tree, single files while fixing, and the whole suite twice on the FINAL tree at the close.
2. **A dated entry in *What executing this plan found*.** It records the tasks, every defect with the measurement that found it, and the negative controls, including which of them could not fail and why. It ends with the gate numbers and the state of the machine, **queried at the close** (`psql`, `docker`, `curl`), never remembered.
3. **This plan's sittings table, updated.** Mark the sitting done and move the `← next` marker. **State no findings count here**: it lives once, in the roadmap's defect-rate table, derived at the CLOSE with the command at the head of *What executing this plan found*.
4. **The close-out sweep in ORIENTATION §6.** Its first line is the roadmap ledger. The gate numbers live in **ORIENTATION's top-of-file box, §2's box, `RUNBOOK.md` and `scripts/ci-acceptance.sh`'s four `EXPECT_` lines**, and all four move together. **Then re-read your own §7e as a cold agent would, and verify every claim by opening what it points at.**

**THIS TABLE IS A SCHEDULE, NOT A CONTRACT.** Moving task boundaries is Task 1's job. **If a measurement breaks the split, say so to Rich before sitting 2 and let him re-cut it. Do not re-cut it silently.** Three rules survive any re-cut. **Task 1 stays first and alone. Task 15 stays alone and last. No task that a spec action changes runs before Rich has decided that spec action** — and if he chooses an option other than the recommended one, the task's own *If Rich chooses otherwise* paragraph says what changes.

## Decided by Rich — build them, do not re-open them

**Each of these was said by Rich on 2026-09-27.** They are recorded in ORIENTATION §8, *Decided*.

- **AGENT MODEL KEYS ARE BROUGHT FORWARD FROM PHASE 3 AND CHARGED TO THE PERSON — OPTION (b) OF THE FRONT-END REVIEW**
  (2026-09-27, in his words: *"I agree with 'Bring agent model keys forward from Phase 3, charged to the person. This needs
  no sandbox or exec, but it is a spec change.'"*). The review offered three answers to *where the building agent runs and
  whose model it uses*: (a) the front-end brings its own provider key — fastest, and outside §10's budgets, attribution and
  D17's classification gate; **(b) this**; (c) wait for Phase 3. **The words the spec needs are Spec action 1**, still his
  to approve; Tasks 9–10 build them.
- **THE FACULTY FRONT-END'S ORIGIN IS `app`** (2026-09-27: *"Let's use app as the entrypoint part of the URL"*) —
  `app.manifest.internal` on the laptop, `app.<production zone>` at UBC. `app` is already a reserved label (§23;
  `infra/reserved-labels/labels.yaml:28`). **How the platform serves two origins is Spec action 2**; Task 8 builds it.
- **ONE PLAN FOR ALL OF IT** (2026-09-27: *"Please can you write one plan which encompasses all of these?"*). The review
  listed what the front-end needs: **Spec action 1's recent-output read, the second origin, the agent's model access, a
  project's display name, binary files, adding members by CWL login, and delete/archive**. This plan replaces the one the
  roadmap placed as *Spec action 1's plan*, in the same position — after the authoring API, ahead of the vulnerability
  database in the console. *The cost, accepted:* one plan of twelve sittings rather than a small one and a large one.
- **BINARY FILES, IN THIS PLAN.** Rich listed them among *"all of these"* on 2026-09-27. **That reverses his *"text files
  only in v1"* of 2026-09-22** (the authoring API plan's Decision 4, which left an optional `encoding` as the additive way
  back). No spec text says *text only*, so there is no spec action. **CONFIRMED by Rich on 2026-09-27, asked directly: *"I
  do mean binary files, not just text files only."*** Task 4 is built.
- **"WRITE THE PLAN WITH YOUR RECOMMENDATIONS FOR ANY ITEMS THAT COME UP"** (2026-09-27, going to bed). So every question
  that came up while writing this was answered with a recommendation, and each is in *Decisions this plan makes* or a spec
  action's *Options*, marked as such, for his morning review.
- **THE REVIEW: THE PLAN, ITS DECISIONS, ITS SPLIT AND ITS FOUR SPEC ACTIONS — ALL APPROVED AS RECOMMENDED** (2026-09-27,
  after sitting 1: *"I've read the plan, all seems good. 12 sittings is fine."*; then, the four spec actions explained to
  him one by one, *"I am happy to go with all of your recommendations. Please make all those changes."*). So all
  thirty-one *Decisions* stand as written; the twelve sittings are the schedule; **Spec actions 1–4 are APPLIED, each as
  option (a)** — Spec action 3 with its sixth edit as drafted (archive and delete synchronous, a second exception to
  D23.9), not option (e)'s `202` — in `1d1afd7`, the four shared HTML pages swept in the close-out that followed; and **the
  agent budget defaults are Decision 22's: $10 a month per person, $2 a session**. No task's *If Rich chooses otherwise*
  paragraph applies.
- **THE FACULTY FRONT-END'S MESSAGE — DECIDED BY RICH IN THE FRONT-END'S OWN SESSION, 2026-09-27, AND CARRIED HERE BY
  SITTING 6.** The front-end is a separate project (§5) at `~/Developer/manifest-app`, served at `app.manifest.internal`
  from 7105, consuming `@manifest/contract` by a pnpm `link:` to this repository's WORKING TREE. Its findings are `FE-1`…
  `FE-25` in `manifest-app/docs/api-findings.md`; the message is `manifest-app/docs/2026-09-27-to-the-platform-session.md`.
  **Never change anything in `manifest-app`** — it is another session's repository. What Rich decided, and where it lands:
  - **FE-2 — the front-end's SERVER may replay the person's session cookie to `GET /v1/me`, to learn who it serves, and for
    nothing else.** `manifest_session` is `Path=/`, and Decision 19 sends every non-API path on `app.` to 7105, so every page
    request delivers the session to that server whether it wants it or not. **Sitting 6 leaves the cookie alone** (done).
    **Sitting 11's *Building a front-end* states the rule**: replay to `getMe` only; never log it, store it or use it for any
    other call — a server can set its own `Origin`, so CSRF does not stop it; the rule is the control. Task 14's *"Two
    credentials, two places"* understates it and is rewritten (its `[S6]`). *Rejected by Rich:* stripping the cookie at the
    edge plus a *"who minted this token"* read; a signed identity statement for the front-end's server.
  - **FE-1 — an INTAKE session with no project, APPROVED IN PRINCIPLE, built in sitting 7**: a platform-paid model key for
    the front-end's intake agents (understanding a description, proposing names checked by `checkSlug`, choosing the
    blueprint and starter), started only by an interactive session, answered once (`withholdOnReplay`), one model named by
    a platform setting that D17 allows for `internal`, bounded per person (recommended $0.25 and 30 minutes a key, 10 keys a
    person a day), confined like every key, audited against the person. Rich: *"the 'understanding what the user asked for,
    proposing names, etc.' can be considered a platform cost. That will always be the same model for all users (and that's
    something we'll need to set as part of our options at the platform level). So I approve of that spec change."* **Its
    words are Spec action 5** (drafted by sitting 6, below — **APPLIED 2026-09-27 (`25e7445`) — Rich approved it as drafted, option (a), in sitting 7's first message, and confirmed so to sitting 6 directly**). **If sitting 7 runs long,
    FE-1 opens the next sitting** (this plan's own rule).
  - **FE-23 — `AgentSession.spentUsd`, built in sitting 7**: faculty see the AI allowance ALWAYS, per conversation (*"$0.40
    so far · $9.60 left this month"*), so *What this plan does not build*'s *"no screen needs it yet"* no longer holds.
    `spentUsd: number | null`, read from LiteLLM `/key/info` by the alias the plan derives (`mf-agent-<id>`), `null` with a
    reason — never `0` — when LiteLLM does not answer, cached like the month.
  - **A CAPABLE MODEL OPTION BESIDE `qwen3.5:4b`**, behind the same LiteLLM, with the `max_classification` D17 allows it;
    `qwen3.5:4b` stays the offline floor (C1); FE-1's intake setting may name the capable one. **Which provider is UBC's
    call**, put to Rich as a decision (ORIENTATION §8 *Open*) and placed in the roadmap — **not this plan's scope** unless he
    says so. Sitting 7 owes one check only: nothing hard-codes a chat name (Decision 23 already reads the catalogue).
  - **FE-24 — STAGING IS UBC'S REAL STAGING WORLD** (the fake IdP is the sandbox's; staging signs real people in against UBC's

    consequence is sitting 10's**: `getInstanceOutput` refuses staging as it refuses production, by a code that names the
    rule, so the recent-output read is sandbox-only (Task 13's `[S6]`).
  - **FE-17, FE-18 and FE-5's sentence, folded into sittings 10–11**: a browser refused at `/auth/*` shown a short page or
    sent to its origin's `/` with a code, not raw JSON (FE-17, Task 13); `@manifest/contract`'s `dist/` complete with its
    types, and a sentence on consuming it from outside the workspace (FE-18, Tasks 13–14); `PendingAction.bodySha256`'s
    canonical form published (FE-5, Task 14).
  - **After this plan, in Rich's order**: the launch path first (FE-6 — the three clocks with D19's drafts, *"I've sent it"*
    and *waiting since*, the staging registration as a tracked object; FE-25 — an owner's *"please sign this off"* feeding
    §26's queue); then FE-19, FE-20, FE-21, FE-22, FE-7 and FE-3; FE-8 to FE-16 are smaller. **FE-4 — nothing notices a live app
    that has died — stays in Phase 4, as specified**; meanwhile **the front-end's server requests every launched app's
    production address once a minute through the edge** (on the public listener) and emails the owners when it stops
    answering.
  - **Keeping in step**: each sitting's close-out lists what it changed in the contract (operations, fields, codes, events)
    and Rich relays it; **`packages/contract` stays buildable at every commit** (the front-end links the working tree).
- **Carried from earlier plans, still binding:** D24's privileged four and the person-only class; step-up for a production
  deploy, an approval, member management and a production secret; the stored preview an approval binds; `SENSITIVE_FIELDS`
  as §7's list; every platform model call whose answer is read or shown is STRUCTURED OUTPUT (the D5 plan's Decision 22 —
  this plan makes none); writes go to `main` only (the authoring API's Decision 9); a path with a `.git` component is refused
  twice; the idempotency fingerprint covers the path parameters (the authoring API's sitting 10, F6).

   action 4 before sitting 5, Spec action 2 before sitting 6, Spec action 1 before sitting 7, Spec action 3 before sitting
   8.** Each carries its exact wording, its options, and a recommendation. **Each is applied to the spec only after he has

## Read this first — what this plan knows that the roadmap and ORIENTATION §7e do not

**Read from the code at `e1712d8` on 2026-09-27, while this plan was written. Task 1 re-measures each one marked *(T1: M<n>)* into `spikes/frontend-baseline/`, where it becomes the record. A plan is a hypothesis.**

1. **THE CONTROL PLANE KNOWS EXACTLY ONE ORIGIN, IN SIX PLACES.** `MANIFEST_CONTROL_PLANE_ORIGIN` (default
   `https://console.manifest.internal`, `config.ts:106-109`) becomes `config.sp.origin` (`:443`, `:568`), and it is read by:
   the CSRF check — a strict `!==` against that one string, and only when `manifest_session` is present (`api/csrf.ts:39-48`),
   called at `api/server.ts:315` and at the stream's upgrade (`api/routes/events.ts:76`, `:95`); the sign-in's return path,
   which accepts a same-origin PATH only (`identity/login-state.ts:21-25`); the platform's SP entity, one ACS and one SLO
   (`sso/platform.ts:76-91`, rendered as `AssertionConsumerService` index 0 alone by `sso/metadata-store.ts:57-70`); the SAML
   client, whose `callbackUrl` is fixed at construction (`identity/saml.ts:231-252`, wired once at `index.ts:371`); and the
   cookies' `secure` flag (`api/routes/auth.ts:78`, `:101`, `:133`). **Every cookie is host-only** (no `domain` anywhere):
   `manifest_session` (`Lax`, 12 h), `manifest_login` and `manifest_stepup` (`Path=/auth`, 600 s, `SameSite=None` on https).
   **So a sign-in begun on `app` today sets `manifest_login` on `app`, the IdP posts to the CONSOLE's ACS, which has no such
   cookie, and the sign-in is refused `SAML_LOGIN_NOT_BOUND`** (`auth.ts:238-255`) — and were it not, the session would be set
   on the console. *(T1: M3.)*
2. **`app.manifest.internal` ALREADY RESOLVES — TO THE WRONG LISTENER.** dnsmasq's parent rule sends the whole zone to
   `127.0.0.3`, the PUBLIC listener, and pins five names back to `127.0.0.2` (`infra/compose.yaml:75-80`: staging, sandbox,
   console, idp, edge). `app.` is not among them, so the host reaches `:8443`'s wildcard, which answers `manifest OK …
   listener=public` (`infra/caddy/Caddyfile:144-147`); inside containers the name reaches srv0, where no site matches and the
   answer is an empty `200` (the `edge.` lesson, `Caddyfile:108-125`). **It needs a pin and its own srv0 site**, as `edge.`
   got in P6a. Nothing listens on **7105**, which is free (`grep -rhoE '\b71[0-9]{2}\b'` over the platform's configuration).
   *(T1: M4.)*
3. **THE DRIVER'S `logs` BOUNDS LINES AND NOT BYTES, AND STAMPS EACH LINE WITH THE TIME IT WAS READ.** `logs(id, opts)`
   takes `{ follow?, tail? }` (`runtime/driver.ts:235-238`, `:345`). `containerLogs` asks Docker for `tail` lines, never
   for `timestamps` (`runtime/docker/logs.ts:42-61`), so ONE line of a megabyte is read whole, and every line's `at` is
   `new Date()` at read time (`:20`, `:38`) — thirty lines printed over an hour read as printed in the same millisecond. The
   fake honours `tail`, ignores `follow` and seeds one line (`runtime/fake-driver.ts:177`, `:267-272`), and the contract
   asserts only that some lines arrive — **not that `tail` is honoured** (`runtime/driver-contract.ts:273-281`). **The Incident's reader is private** (`readLogTail`,
   `observability/incidents.ts:173-196`), keeps the LAST 200 lines in a sliding window, and is redacted by the caller with
   `makeRedactor(secretValues)` (`observability/redact.ts:127-151`: the app's secret values, longest first, then five
   patterns and an entropy rule), fed by `deps.appSecrets.secretValues(db, { projectId, environmentKind })`
   (`releases/release.ts:583-588`). *(T1: M1, M2.)*
4. **INSTANCES ARE KEPT, AND NOTHING LISTS THEM.** Retired rows go to `destroying` then `gone` and are never deleted
   (`releases/retire.ts:109-112`, `:176-181`). `getEnvironment` answers ONE instance — the one the route reaches, else the
   newest by `lastSeenAt` (`projects/repository.ts:258-276`). `instances.handle` is the driver's id (`release.ts:796`), and the
   representation never publishes it (`api/representations/instances.ts:33`). The table has **no `created_at`**
   (`db/schema.ts:244-261`), so *newest* is `last_seen_at`. **This is the authoring API's sitting 10, F13.**
5. **THE CONTROL PLANE NEVER READS AI SPEND, AND EVERY KEY IT MINTS IS OPEN-ENDED.** The admin client calls `/user/new`
   (with `auto_create_key: false` — 1.98.0 defaults it to true, `ai/keys.ts:66`), `/user/update`, `/key/generate`,
   `/key/delete` (404 read as done, `:240`), `/model/info` and, for release summaries, `/chat/completions`. **No spend
   endpoint** anywhere in `src/` (`projects/fleet.ts:37` and `api/representations/fleet.ts:62` say *"Not yet … AI spend"*).
   App keys carry **no `duration`, no `key_alias` and no key budget** — the budget is on the per-project user `mf-<projectId>-
   <kind>` (`keys.ts:45-78`, `:126-137`); `allowed_routes` is the frozen three (`:21-25`); **an empty model list means EVERY
   model** (`:117`). The catalogue's ranks are public 0 < internal 1 < confidential 2 (`spec/schema.ts:12-16`), a model is
   allowed when its rank is at least the app's (`spec/policy.ts:163`, `:207`; re-checked at deploy, `release.ts:1074-1083`),
   and **§7's default classification is `internal`** (`spec/schema.ts:259-262`). LiteLLM listens on **`127.0.0.1:7106`**
   (`infra/compose.yaml:219`; `config.ts:252`) — reachable from any host process. *(T1: M7, M8.)*
6. **THE ORPHAN SCRIPT WOULD DELETE EVERY AGENT BUDGET.** `scripts/litellm-orphans.sh` holds a LiteLLM user only if one of
   its keys hashes to an `LLM_API_KEY` some `mf-*-app` container carries (`:78-95`, `:131-140`); every other user but
   `default_user_id` is an orphan, and `--apply` deletes its keys and then the user. **A person's agent user is held by no
   container**, so the first cleanup after Task 9 would erase every person's month of spend — which is what a budget IS.
7. **A PROJECT ROW CAN NEVER BE DELETED.** `audit.events.project_id`, `audit.build_logs.build_id` and
   `audit.incidents.instance_id` are `RESTRICT` (`db/schema.ts:871-873`, `:926-928`, `:967-969`), and the application role
   holds `SELECT` and `INSERT` on the audit schema only (`drizzle/0004`, `0005`, `0006`; §20 *Audit integrity*). The table's
   own comment says so: *"Nothing in the control plane deletes a project, so RESTRICT costs nothing today"* (`:867-869`).
   `pending_actions.requested_by_token` is `RESTRICT` too, with a comment addressed to this plan (`:415-420`). **So
   *delete* is a tombstone, not a `DELETE`** — Spec action 3 says so in the spec's words. The only `deleteProject` today
   rolls back a creation that failed before any event existed (`projects/repository.ts:118-126`).
8. **THE TEARDOWN PRIMITIVES EXIST, AND NONE HAS A PROJECT-LEVEL CALLER.** `retireEnvironment` retires what is NOT serving
   and, when nothing serves, deliberately does nothing (`releases/retire.ts:54`, `:71-80`); the driver's `retireInstance`
   refuses a serving instance (`runtime/docker/driver.ts:612-618`) — **so taking an app down is: point its hostname
   elsewhere FIRST, then retire what no longer serves.** `destroyService(id, { deleteData })` (`runtime/docker/services.ts:
   153-166`) keeps or removes the `-data` volume and has **no production caller**; `destroyAppNetwork`
   (`runtime/docker/networks.ts:142`) and `destroyEgressProxy` (`runtime/docker/egress.ts:171`) are not on the `Driver`
   interface and have no caller; `deleteSpRow` (`sso/metadata-store.ts:182`) is used by tests only; `SourceDriver.
   destroyRepository` exists on both drivers (`source/git-driver.ts:307`; `local-driver.ts:386`; `github/driver.ts:642`,
   `:907`) and only the contract calls it; there is deliberately **no `revokeAppKey`** (`ai/keys.ts:89-90`); `removeRoute`
   (`routing/routes.ts:73`) and the route's `@id` (`:73-79`) are how a hostname's route is addressed. *(T1: M9.)*
9. **`project:delete` IS A CAPABILITY NO ROUTE ASSERTS, IT IS MINTABLE TODAY, AND THE MATRIX DEPENDS ON THAT.** Owner and
   administrator hold it; the collaborator does not (`projects/authz.ts:326`, `:338-341`); it is in neither `PRIVILEGED`
   (`:93-98`) nor `PERSON_ONLY` (`:116-119`) nor `STEP_UP_GUARDED`, so the mint route accepts it. **And
   `api/authz-contract.ts:1849-1853` builds the `token-incapable` actor from `['project:delete']` PRECISELY BECAUSE no route
   asserts it** — the first route that does (Task 11) turns that actor's meaning inside out.
10. **A PERSON IS KNOWN BY THEIR PUID, AND ONLY BY IT.** `users` holds `ubc_cwl_puid` (unique), `email`, `display_name`, `role`
    (`db/schema.ts:45-52`). The control plane asks the IdP for `ubcEduCwlPuid`, `mail`, `givenName` and `sn` only
    (`sso/platform.ts:53`) and reads nothing else (`identity/saml.ts:411-447`, stored at `:467-494`); `uid`'s OID is known
    (`sso/attributes.ts:27`) and asked for by nobody. The local IdP's test users are `student` (`stu000001`), `instructor`
    (`ins000001`) and `operator` (`opr000001`), **none of them carrying `uid`** (`infra/idp/config/authsources.php:23-46`).
    `addMember` looks up `ubc_cwl_puid` alone and answers `400 MEMBER_USER_NOT_FOUND` for anyone who never signed in
    (`api/routes/project-reads.ts:337-406`) — **and publishes no event**, as `removeMember` publishes none (ORIENTATION §3).
    *(T1: M10.)*
11. **THE WRITE PATH IS ALREADY BYTE-SAFE UNDER ITS TEXT RULES.** `buildCommit` writes each blob's BYTES to a numbered
    scratch file and hashes them with one `git hash-object -w --no-filters --stdin-paths` (`source/plumbing.ts:276-288`);
    the only text step is `Buffer.from(change.content, 'utf8')` (`:132`) and the request's three refinements — a lone
    surrogate, a NUL, 1 MiB of UTF-8 (`api/representations/source.ts:215-240`). **But both push-time scans SKIP a binary
    file** — *"A binary file has no hunk, so it is skipped"* (`source/scan-commits.ts:80-81`; the rendered `pre-receive`
    shares `PATCH_ARGS`, `source/pre-receive.ts:4`, `:33`, `:100`) — **so a binary write is a way past the secret scan unless
    the API scans it itself.** The build gate reads every file of 2 MB or less as UTF-8 and runs `scanText`
    (`build/gates.ts:54-70`). The read path refuses a NUL in the first 8000 bytes or bad UTF-8 as `409 SOURCE_FILE_NOT_TEXT`
    (`source/reading.ts:193-201`). `createCommit`'s `bodyLimit` is 8 MiB (`api/routes/source.ts:466`) and the edge's
    `request_body` is **10 MB** (`Caddyfile:53-55`). *(T1: M11.)*
12. **THE CONTRACT IS `1.3.0`, WITH 54 OPERATIONS, 78 SCHEMAS, 111 ERROR CODES AND 37 EVENT TYPES**; the API already
    speaks `PATCH` (`api/contract/route.ts:21`; `api/server.ts:187`'s `MUTATING`) and has never served one. An additive
    change bumps the minor, once per plan: **this plan takes `1.4.0`**, at the first task whose change reaches
    `openapi.json` — **predicted: Task 3**, because Task 2 changes nothing published (`LogLine` is the driver's, not
    the API's). *(T1: M13.)*
13. **`withholdOnReplay` IS HOW AN ANSWER CARRYING A CREDENTIAL IS KEPT OUT OF THE IDEMPOTENCY RECORD** (`api/contract/
    route.ts`; `mintToken`'s at `api/routes/tokens.ts:80-91`, which answers a replay `409 TOKEN_ALREADY_MINTED` naming the
    token and never its secret). **A model key is a credential**; `startAgentSession` declares the same.
14. **THE CONSOLE'S COVERAGE GATE AND THE MOCK'S ARE BOTH LIVE.** `DELIBERATELY_UNCALLED` is empty
    (`packages/console/src/coverage.test.ts:19-40`) and refuses a stale entry; the mock answers an operation from the
    document's own example when `ANSWERS` has no fixture for it (`packages/mock/src/server.ts:107-118`, `:126`). **A capability
    added to `CAPABILITIES` is a `tsc` error in the console** until `packages/console/src/screens/tokens.tsx`'s
    `everyCapability([...])` names it (the authoring API's `[S4]` 2).
15. **THE BASELINE**: `pnpm test` **2330 passed in 162 files**, `pnpm test:docker` **210 in 35**, `make doctor` **20/0/0**,
    `make verify` **57/0/0**, **33 migrations** (`0000`–`0032`), `make ci-acceptance` every step PASS on driver 1
    (ORIENTATION §2, 2026-09-27). **This plan's starting line, kept as written; the CURRENT baseline is always ORIENTATION
    §2's box.** *(T1: M14.)*

## Decisions this plan makes, and why

**Thirty-one questions below Rich's line, made while the plan was written as recommendations for his morning review — AND ALL THIRTY-ONE APPROVED BY HIM AS WRITTEN ON 2026-09-27** (*Decided by Rich*, its last bullet). Each records what it rejected and what changing course would cost.

### Recent output (Tasks 2–3)

**Decision 1. ONE reader and ONE redactor, shared with the Incident.** `observability/output.ts` exports `readRecentOutput(source, handle, bounds)`, which answers the last lines as `{ lines: OutputLine[], truncated: { lines, bytes }, failure? }`. `incidents.ts`'s private `readLogTail` becomes a caller of it that joins the lines. The route and the Incident both redact with `makeRedactor(await appSecrets.secretValues(db, scope))`. **§14's *"redacted at read with the rules that redact `Incident.log_tail`"* is then one code path**, and a change to one is a change to the other. *Rejected:* **a second reader in the route** — two sliding windows, two bounds, and the first difference between them is a leak that reads as a feature.

**Decision 2. The output's bounds: 200 lines by default, at most 1000; 256 KiB in all; each line cut at 4 KiB.** `?lines=` asks for fewer or more within the cap. A line longer than 4 KiB is cut and ends `…[cut: N bytes]`, so one minified bundle printed on crash cannot fill the answer.
- **The bound is enforced in two places, and neither is a byte limit on the READ.** The driver CUTS each line at `lineBytes` as it demultiplexes (`LogOpts.lineBytes`), so a megabyte line is never buffered. The reader then keeps the NEWEST lines that fit in 256 KiB, dropping from the oldest end.
- **A driver that stopped reading at a byte limit would stop at the OLDEST end of the tail** — Docker sends a tail oldest first — and drop exactly the lines anyone wants. (This paragraph's first draft did that; the self-review caught it.)
- The reader asks for `lines + 1`, so `truncated.lines` is known rather than guessed. `truncated.lines` and `truncated.bytes` say which bound was met. *Rejected:* **lines only** — *Read this first* 3 is the reason. **A byte bound alone** — a client asking for *"the last 50 lines"* is the natural question, and the Incident already asks it. *Changing course* is three numbers.

**Decision 3. Docker's own timestamps, not the time of reading.** `LogOpts` gains `timestamps?: boolean`, and `containerLogs` asks Docker for them and parses each line's RFC 3339 prefix into `at`. A line Docker could not stamp keeps the read time, and says so in a boolean (`at` is still required). The fake stores a time per line. *Rejected:* **read time** (*Read this first* 3) — an agent correlating *"the request at 10:03"* with a line needs the line's own time, and the Incident's `log_tail` stays a string, unchanged. *(T1: M2 measures the prefix's exact shape on this Docker.)*

**Decision 4. Two operations: `listInstances` and `getInstanceOutput`.** `GET /v1/environments/{environmentId}/instances` answers the environment's instances, newest `lastSeenAt` first, **at most 50**, each with `serving: boolean`, and `truncated`. It needs `project:read`, as `getEnvironment` does. `GET /v1/instances/{instanceId}/output?lines=` answers `InstanceOutput`. **An instance is named by its id**, which `getEnvironment`, `deploy` and `listInstances` all answer — which is how a client names one (§7e's question). *Rejected:* **`/v1/environments/{id}/output`, reading the serving instance implicitly** — the instance an agent wants is often the one that FAILED, which is not serving. **Adding instances to `Environment`** — a list in a resource that is read on every screen.

**Decision 5. Reading output is its own capability, `output:read`** — owner, collaborator and administrator; mintable; neither privileged nor person-only (§14: *"a delegated token holding the capability for it"*). **Not `project:read`**: sandbox and staging output carries what test users typed, and a person minting a read-only token for a dashboard should not hand that over by default. *Rejected:* **reusing `project:read`**, which is §14's words ignored. *Changing course* is the matrix.

**Decision 6. Production is refused by its OWN code, before the driver is asked.** `403 INSTANCE_OUTPUT_PRODUCTION` — the capability is held, the environment is the reason — from the environment row, **never** from anything the request names. An instance with no handle, or one `destroying` or `gone`, is `409 INSTANCE_OUTPUT_UNAVAILABLE` (its container is gone; its Incident, if it failed, has the last lines). *Rejected:* **`404` for production**, which would say the instance does not exist when every other read says it does. **Reading output publishes no event**: a read, like `getBuildLog`.

### Binary files (Task 4)

**Decision 7. A write names its `encoding`; `utf8` is the default and `base64` is new.** `WriteChange` gains `encoding?: 'utf8' | 'base64'`. With `base64`, `content` must be **canonical** base64 (no whitespace, correct padding — re-encoding the decoded bytes gives back the string), decoding to **at most 2 MiB**. `getFile` gains `?encoding=base64`, which answers ANY file up to 2 MiB — text or binary — and `SourceFile` gains `encoding` on every answer (`utf8` unless asked). **A text read is unchanged**: still 1 MiB and `SOURCE_FILE_NOT_TEXT` for binary. *Rejected:* **a separate upload endpoint** (multipart) — a commit is atomic, and a logo committed separately from the page that shows it is two commits an agent must keep in step. **Raising the per-commit body limit** — the edge's 10 MB is the ceiling (*Read this first* 11), and 8 MiB of JSON holds two files at the limit plus text. *Changing course* is one number.

**Decision 8. Bytes sent as base64 must NOT be text.** A decoded payload that is valid UTF-8 with no NUL in its first 8000 bytes — git's own rule, and the read path's — is refused `400 REQUEST_INVALID` naming the change's index: *"this is text; send it with `encoding: 'utf8'`"*. **Otherwise base64 is a way to put a script past the text rules, the diff and the secret scan's hunk reader.**

**Decision 9. Binary content is confined to ten media types, recognised by their first bytes** — PNG, JPEG, GIF, WebP, ICO, PDF, WOFF, WOFF2, TrueType and OpenType. Anything else is `400 REQUEST_INVALID` naming the rule and the path. **What a course app needs — its images, a syllabus, a typeface — and nothing an app can EXECUTE**: no ELF, no Mach-O, no archive, no WebAssembly. A person's `git push` can still put anything in the repository; this confines what an AGENT puts there through the API (D14). *Rejected:* **any bytes** — the build copies the repository into the image, and an agent that can commit a native binary can ship one. **A library** (`file-type`) — a dependency for ten patterns (*Tech Stack*). *Changing course* is one table.

**Decision 10. Binary content is scanned for secrets by its printable runs.** Every run of at least 16 printable ASCII bytes — `strings(1)`'s rule with a longer minimum — goes through `scanText` as lines. A hit is `409 SOURCE_SECRET_DETECTED`, with `repository.secret_refused` naming the path and rule and never the value, exactly as for text. **This closes *Read this first* 11's hole for the API's own writes**; a person's push of a binary stays unscanned at push time and is still read by the build gate. *Rejected:* **no scan** — a PDF can carry a pasted key in plain ASCII, and the push-time scans would never see it.

### What the authoring API hands over (Task 5)

**Decision 11. F4 is the console's wording, not a new `madeThrough` kind.** The seed commit — the one with no parent — is read as *"the project's starting point, made by Manifest"*. `madeThrough` stays `null` for it, as published. *Rejected:* **`madeThrough.kind: 'platform'`** — widening an enum on a response is a change every generated client's exhaustive `switch` meets, for one commit per project.

**Decision 12. F9's caps: `getCommit` answers at most 1000 changes, and `getTree` learns which files are binary for the paths it LISTS, never the whole tree.**
- `CommitDetail` gains `truncated`.
- Today `binaryPaths` runs `git diff --numstat` of the empty tree against the whole commit (`source/reading.ts:82-96`), so a 200,000-file tree costs a 200,000-file diff even though the listing stops at 10,000 entries. The diff is now limited to the listed paths (`--pathspec-from-file` over the ≤10,000 it answers).
- *Rejected:* **dropping the flag** — a client uses it to say *"a binary file — not editable here"*, and Task 4's `encoding=base64` read is what it offers instead.
- *(T1: M12 measures both on a 200,000-file tree, and whether `--pathspec-from-file` takes 10,000 paths on git 2.50.1.)*

**Decisions for F7, F8, F10, F11 and F12 are the findings' own remedies**, stated in Task 5.

### A project's name, and people (Tasks 6–7)

**Decision 13. `name` is free text of 1 to 80 characters, trimmed, with no control character.** It is set at creation — the slug when none is given — and changed by `PATCH /v1/projects/{projectId}` with `{ name }`, which needs `project:write` (mintable). It is published as `project.renamed` `{ from, to }`. **It never reaches a hostname, an SP entity, a Docker name or anything else §23 derives from the slug.** *Rejected:* **renaming the slug** — D26 and §9 make it permanent after launch, and a rename before launch touches every derived name at once.

**Decision 14. People are found by `cwlLogin`, `email` or `puid` — exactly one.** `AddMemberRequest` keeps `puid` and gains `cwlLogin` and `email`, each optional, **exactly one present** (`400 REQUEST_INVALID` otherwise) — additive, so today's clients are unchanged. `cwlLogin` matches the stored login exactly, lowercased. `email` matches case-insensitively, and **two people sharing one address is `400 MEMBER_USER_AMBIGUOUS`** — a `BadRequestError` like its sibling `MEMBER_USER_NOT_FOUND`, because the request named nobody in particular — naming neither. Nobody found is today's `MEMBER_USER_NOT_FOUND`, whose message names what was looked for and nothing else. *Rejected:* **a people search** — a directory of everyone who has signed in, readable by every owner, is a privacy surface the PIA would have to describe; a lookup of ONE name the owner already knows is not.

**Decision 15. `uid` is asked for, kept, and published as `cwlLogin` on a member.** `CONTROL_PLANE_ATTRIBUTES` gains `uid`, `toIdentity` reads it (optional — an assertion without it still signs in, with `cwl_login` left null), and `users.cwl_login` is unique where not null. The local IdP's three test users gain `uid` equal to their login. **Membership changes publish `member.added` and `member.removed`**, the sentence naming the person, because a People screen has nothing else to learn from. *(**As built, sitting 5 (2026-09-27):** `member.added` is `{ memberId, role, previousRole, via, userId, tokenId }` and `member.removed` `{ memberId, via, userId, tokenId }` — `userId` keeps its meaning in every other event, WHO ACTED, so the person the change is about is `memberId`; neither is published for a request that changed nothing. And the login FOLLOWS ITS HOLDER: a sign-in clears it from any other row first, and an assertion without `uid` leaves a stored one alone — the sitting's F1 and F2.)* *Rejected:* **requiring `uid`** — Manifest's own UBC registration does not ask for it yet (*What Rich does* 6), and a hard requirement would lock out every person until it does.

### The `app` origin (Task 8)

**Decision 16. Origins are a configured list, and a request is judged against the one it ARRIVED on.** `MANIFEST_CONTROL_PLANE_ORIGIN` stays (the console's, and the first). `MANIFEST_FRONTEND_ORIGIN` is new — default `https://app.manifest.internal`, **empty to disable**. `config.origins` holds both. `originOf(request, origins)` is the configured origin whose host equals the request's `Host`, which the edge preserves (P5c Task 1, M1), **or the first when none matches** (the Docker tier's loopback control planes). **CSRF then asks `Origin === originOf(request)`, not *"one of the list"***: a page on the console cannot post to the `app` origin's API with the `app` cookie, and each origin stays exactly as same-origin as the console alone was. *Rejected:* **membership in the list** — it admits a cross-origin post between Manifest's own two origins, which nothing needs.

**Decision 17. One SP entity, one assertion-consumer URL and one SAML client per origin.** `controlPlaneSpEntity` answers `endpoints: [{ origin, acsUrl, sloUrl }]` — one for each origin, the console's first. `renderSpMetadata` writes one `AssertionConsumerService` per endpoint (`index` 0, 1…) and one `SingleLogoutService` (the first's — SimpleSAMLphp answers a logout to the first of a binding, *T1: M5*). `createSamlSp` is called ONCE PER ORIGIN, each with its own `callbackUrl`, and `deps.samlSp` becomes `deps.samlSpFor(origin)`. **The AuthnRequest names its origin's ACS, and the IdP accepts it only because the row lists it** (D15's reasoning: the origin is Manifest's, never a request's). *(T1: M3 measures SimpleSAMLphp honouring `AssertionConsumerServiceURL` for index 1.)* *Rejected:* **two SP entities** — two UBC IAM registrations for one platform, and two keypairs to rotate. **Signing in on the console and handing the browser to `app`** — a one-time credential in a redirect is a new credential class, and it is the thing §20's binding of a sign-in to its browser exists to refuse.

**Decision 18. A sign-out returns to the origin it began on.** `POST /auth/logout` on `app` clears `app`'s cookie and answers the IdP's SLO URL with `RelayState` naming `app`'s origin. The IdP's `LogoutResponse` lands on the FIRST endpoint's `/auth/logout` (the console's), which now redirects to the `RelayState`'s origin **when it is one of `config.origins`, and to `/` otherwise**. *(T1: M5.)*

**Decision 19. The edge serves `app.manifest.internal` exactly as it serves `console.`**: `/v1/*` and `/auth/*` to 7100, everything else to **7105**, the same headers, the same `@outside` guard, the same `stream_close_delay 1h`, and a dnsmasq pin to `127.0.0.2`. **Nothing in this repository serves 7105 for real** — the front-end is a separate project (§5), and until it exists 7105 answers `502`, which `make doctor` and `make verify` tolerate exactly as they tolerate the console's (ORIENTATION §3). **For the clicked half, the reference console can be served there** — `pnpm --filter @manifest/console preview --port 7105` with `app.manifest.internal` in `allowedHosts` — because it is the one client in this repository that signs a person in through a browser.

### Agent sessions (Tasks 9–10)

**Decision 20. An agent session is a row, and its key is never stored.** Table `agent_sessions` has these columns:
- `id`, `project_id`, `user_id` (the person charged);
- `requested_by_token` (null for a session);
- `name` — a person's label for the task;
- `models` (jsonb), `cap_usd` (numeric), `expires_at`, `created_at`;
- `ended_at` and `end_reason` — `ended`, `token_revoked`, `project_archived` or `project_deleted`.

**The key's alias is `mf-agent-<id>`**, which is what `/key/delete` names. It is DERIVED from the id (`agentKeyAlias`), not stored, so it cannot disagree with it.

**The key's plaintext is in `startAgentSession`'s answer and nowhere else**, like a token's secret, and the route declares `withholdOnReplay`. A replay answers `409 AGENT_SESSION_ALREADY_STARTED`, naming the session. *Rejected:* **storing the key encrypted so it can be shown again** — a credential shown twice is a credential in two places.

**Decision 21. A LiteLLM user per PERSON carries the monthly budget; the key carries the session's cap and its life.** The user is `mf-person-<userId>`, with `max_budget` = the person's monthly agent budget and `budget_duration: 1mo`, created or updated at each start (`ensureAiUser`'s shape). The key is `/key/generate` with:
- `user_id` — that person's user;
- `key_alias`;
- `models` — Decision 23's list;
- `allowed_routes` — the frozen three;
- `duration` — `<seconds>s`;
- `max_budget` — the session cap;
- `metadata` — `{ manifest_project, manifest_agent_session, manifest_person, manifest_token }`.

**Two budgets at two levels, because D8's *"a looping agent burns its own cap"* is the session's, and *"charged to the person"* is the month's.** *(T1: M7 measures a key `max_budget` and `duration` refusing on 1.98.0.)*

**Decision 22. The numbers:**
- **The monthly agent budget** is `MANIFEST_AGENT_MONTHLY_USD`, default **10**.
- **The session cap** is `MANIFEST_AGENT_SESSION_CAP_USD`, default **2**. A request may ask for less. It is never more than what remains of the month.
- **The life** is `durationMinutes`, default **60**, at most **480**. **It is never beyond the requesting token's own expiry**, so a key cannot outlive the credential that asked for it by waiting.
- **A month already spent is `409 AGENT_BUDGET_EXHAUSTED`**, before anything is minted.
- *Rejected:* **a per-person column now** — §6's `User.agent_monthly_usd` (Spec action 1) is an administrator's to set, and this plan builds no administrator's route for it (§26), so a column nothing writes would be the no-caller shape. The default binds everyone until the admin console lands.

**Decision 23. The models are the ones D17 allows for the project's classification, with production as a floor.** The classification is the newest VALID manifest's, or §7's default `internal` when there is none. **Once launched, it is never less restrictive than the classification of the release serving production.** **Every catalogue entry whose `max_classification` rank is at least that is allowed.** If none is, the answer is `409 AGENT_NO_MODEL_FOR_CLASSIFICATION`, before anything is minted: an empty list means EVERY model to LiteLLM (*Read this first* 5).
- **The production floor exists because the agent writes `manifest.yaml`.** Without it, an agent could lower `data.classification` in a commit and start its next session on an off-premise model, while production — whose change would re-escalate — still says `confidential`.
- *Rejected:* **an administrator's agent catalogue** — a second list to keep in step with D17's, deciding the same question.

**Decision 24. `agent:session` is a new capability** — owner, collaborator and administrator hold it; it is mintable, neither privileged nor person-only. `getAgentBudget` answers the budget of **the person the credential acts for** — the session's person, or a token's minter — at `GET /v1/agent-budget`, which any credential may call. **Not under `/v1/me`**: `getMe` is a session's alone, and a token that runs out of budget must be able to say so to its person. Spend comes from LiteLLM's `/user/info` for `mf-person-<userId>` (*T1: M8*), is cached for 10 s, and is **answered as `null` with a reason when LiteLLM does not answer** — never as `0`.

**Decision 25. A session ends in exactly four ways, and each revokes its key by alias:**
- `endAgentSession`. The person may end any of their project's sessions; a token may end the ones it started.
- The token that started it being revoked — `revokeToken` ends them first.
- The project being archived or deleted.
- Its `duration` running out, which LiteLLM enforces. The row reads as expired from `expires_at`, exactly as the queue screen reads a lapsed question (P5c Decision 8). No timer.

`agent_session.started` and `agent_session.ended` are published, the sentence naming the person and, for a token, its name. **A key's revocation that fails is an operator line and a `500`, never a swallowed catch** — a live key nobody can end is the one outcome worse than a refusal.

**Decision 26. The orphan script learns what a person's budget is.** `mf-person-*` users are HELD while they hold a key LiteLLM reports unexpired, and are otherwise orphans — **which resets that person's month on this laptop**, and the script's own output says so before `--apply`. *Rejected:* **reading the control database from the script** — every cleanup script re-derives from what Docker and LiteLLM report, by design (ORIENTATION §3).

### Archive, restore and delete (Tasks 11–12)

**Decision 27. `projects.state` is `active`, `archived` or `deleted`**, with `archived_at` and `deleted_at`, and **`assertCapability` refuses a project that is not active for every capability except `project:read` and `project:delete`.** Its session branch already selects the project row, for administrators and members alike (`projects/authz.ts`, `select({ id: projects.id })`), so it selects `state` too and a person's session pays nothing new. The refusal is `409 PROJECT_ARCHIVED`, and a deleted project is the stranger's `404 NOT_FOUND`. **A token cannot reach an archived project at all**, because archiving revokes every token first — so the per-request cost P5b's Decision 9 rejected is never paid. *Rejected:* **a guard per route** — the first route written after this plan would forget it.

**Decision 28. Archive is a sequence of idempotent steps, recorded as done, re-run at boot.** The state goes to `archived` FIRST, which stops new work. Then:
1. every agent session ended;
2. every token revoked;
3. every pending action expired;
4. each environment — its hostname pointed at the SWITCHED-OFF route, then every instance not `gone` retired (with the usual drain), then its services stopped **keeping their data**, then its network and egress proxy removed;
5. the sandbox and staging SP rows deleted;
6. `project.archived` published.

**A step that fails answers `500 PROJECT_TEARDOWN_INCOMPLETE`**, with an operator line naming the step, and leaves the project archived. **The same request retried continues**, and `recoverAtBoot` re-runs the teardown of every archived project, because an edge restart drops runtime routes and a crash mid-way leaves containers. *Rejected:* **a background job** — Phase 4's reconciler is where one lives (D10); synchronous, like a deploy, is what exists. **That makes archive and delete a second exception to D23.9, which names a deploy as its only one** — Spec action 3's sixth edit says so in the spec, and its option (e) is the `202` alternative.

**Decision 29. A switched-off hostname answers `410 Gone` with a short platform page** — *"This app has been switched off by its owner."* — through a Caddy route carrying a `static_response` under the hostname's own route id. **So the next deploy after a restore replaces it in place** (`applyRoute` addresses a route by `@id`), and an archived app's name never falls through to the wildcard's `manifest OK` or to another app. *Rejected:* **removing the route** — the name then answers the platform's probe text to a student. **`503`** — it invites a retry that will never succeed.

**Decision 30. Archive, restore and delete are person-only, and archive and delete need step-up.** `project:delete` joins `PERSON_ONLY` (the mint route refuses it, and `assertCapability` refuses any token holding it), and its three routes declare `credential: 'session'`. `archiveProject` and `deleteProject` call `assertStepUp`, and `project:delete` joins `STEP_UP_GUARDED`; `restoreProject` does not, because bringing an app back takes nothing from anyone. **`token-incapable` in the authorization matrix moves to `['quota:set']`** — a capability no route asserts, which is privileged, so the mint route refuses it and the store holds it (*Read this first* 9). *If Rich chooses Spec action 3's option (b) or (c), Task 11 says what changes.*

**Decision 31. Delete is only for a project that never launched, and it leaves a tombstone.** `DELETE /v1/projects/{projectId}` archives first if the project is active. It then:
- destroys each service's data volume (`destroyService({ deleteData: true })`);
- deletes every row in `secrets` for the project;
- destroys the repository (`SourceDriver.destroyRepository` — on driver 2 that deletes the GitHub repository);
- deletes the per-project LiteLLM users;
- removes the switched-off routes, so the names answer nothing of this project's;
- sets `state = 'deleted'` and publishes `project.deleted`.

**The row and its audit trail stay** (*Read this first* 7). The slug is released by making `projects_slug_key` a partial unique index, `WHERE state <> 'deleted'`. A deleted project answers `404` to every route, the administrator's included.

**A launched project is refused `409 PROJECT_LAUNCHED_NOT_DELETABLE`**, because its data's disposal is `data.retention_days` and UBC's sunset procedure (§9, §19), and its canonical hostname is permanent (D26).

**Registry images are not deleted** — the laptop's registry does not enable deletion, and a recreated slug's images are tagged by different commits (*What this plan does not build*).

---

## Global Constraints

Every task's requirements implicitly include this section. Values are copied verbatim from the spec, or from a dated measurement.

- **Four gates, all clean before every commit**, from the **repository root**: `pnpm test`, `pnpm lint`, `pnpm typecheck` and `pnpm format:check`. **Run `pnpm test` twice** on the final tree of a sitting, because a suite that is not repeatable has a state leak. Never run it with `--filter`. **The baseline is *Read this first* 15**; the CURRENT baseline is always ORIENTATION §2's box. `pnpm test` is ~9 minutes a run (525–531 s on 2026-09-27).
- **`pnpm test:docker`** (~16 min, needs `make up` and the chat model warm, **fails rather than skips**) is owed by any change to `runtime/`, `routing/`, `services/`, `build/`, `releases/`, `identity/`, `sso/`, `secrets/`, `projects/`, `blueprints/`, `spec/`, `ai/`, `observability/`, `launch/`, `source/`, `infra/`, or any `*.docker.test.ts`. **It outlasts the agent's 10-minute tool limit: run it in the background.** It restarts the edge, truncates the tables and re-registers the platform's SP row — **restart the control plane afterwards**, then run the three cleanup scripts, then `make verify` (twice in the D5 plan it left the host unable to reach the edge; `docker restart manifest-caddy` fixed it). Warm the model first: `curl -s http://127.0.0.1:11434/api/generate -d '{"model":"qwen3.5:4b","prompt":"ok","stream":false,"think":false,"keep_alive":"30m","options":{"num_predict":1}}'`.
- **`pnpm test -- <filter>` does not filter.** One file, from the repository root: a unit file `pnpm exec vitest run --project unit src/<path>`; a packages file `pnpm exec vitest run --project packages packages/<pkg>/src/<path>`; a Docker file `MANIFEST_TEST_DOCKER=1 pnpm exec vitest run --project docker src/<path>`. **Never run two Vitest processes at once.**
- **`pnpm test` TRUNCATES the control plane's tables** — so does one file, and so does `pnpm contract:write`. **Run anything that needs a demo's rows BEFORE any Vitest run.**
- **Vitest strips types; it does not check them.** `pnpm typecheck` is the only gate that sees a whole class of error. `exactOptionalPropertyTypes` is on: an optional field is a conditional spread.
- **A route change is three files, in order**: the definition, then `pnpm contract:write`, then `pnpm contract:generate`. **`packages/contract/openapi.json` and `src/schema.d.ts` are generated; never edit them.** Registering an error code, an event type or a representation field moves the document too. **Every new operation needs `examples` captured from its own tests** (the reference's gate, `api/contract/docs.test.ts`, parses each through its schema), **a row in `api/authz-contract.ts`**, **a step in `packages/journey/src/coverage.ts`'s `JOURNEY`** (or an `OUTSIDE_THE_JOURNEY` line with its reason), and **either a caller in `packages/console/src/api.ts` or a `DELIBERATELY_UNCALLED` line naming Task 13 as its remover**.
- **A migration is `pnpm --filter @manifest/control-plane db:generate`, then READ what it wrote.** Drizzle rewrites the `audit.events` `CHECK` itself when `db/schema.ts`'s literal changes; **appending it by hand makes the migration fail to apply**. **The migration must be APPLIED to `manifest_control` before the unit tier runs** — the harness truncates and never migrates, so a new `CHECK` literal is a `500` on every insert until `db:migrate` runs (the authoring API's `[S4]` 4; ORIENTATION §3 has the one-line form). **Migration numbers here are predictions** (`0033` onwards); take what drizzle writes. **A custom migration** (Task 12's partial index, Task 6's backfill) is `db:generate --custom`, written by hand, and read back with `\d` in `psql`.
- **An EVENT TYPE is four edits and published surface**: `EVENT_TYPES` (`observability/events.ts`), its payload schema (`observability/event-schemas.ts`), the `CHECK` literal in `db/schema.ts`, and `EXAMPLE_DETAILS` (`observability/examples.ts`) — **with a `.describe()` sentence on its map entry and every field**, which the reference's gate refuses without — **and it must be REACHED by `api/stream-contract.test.ts`'s lifecycle or listed in its `PUBLISHED_ELSEWHERE`** (the authoring API's `[S4]` 3). **A payload never carries a secret's value, a model key, a token, a file's content, an output line or a laptop path.** A sentence names a person by name, never by PUID.
- **EVERY REFUSAL ASSERTS ITS CODE, NEVER ITS STATUS ALONE** — `refusal()` from `api/testing.ts`. This plan adds **`403 INSTANCE_OUTPUT_PRODUCTION`** and **`409 INSTANCE_OUTPUT_UNAVAILABLE`** (family `OutputError`, Task 3), **`400 MEMBER_USER_AMBIGUOUS`** (`BadRequestError`, beside `MEMBER_USER_NOT_FOUND`, Task 7), **`409 AGENT_SESSION_ALREADY_STARTED`**, **`409 AGENT_BUDGET_EXHAUSTED`** and **`409 AGENT_NO_MODEL_FOR_CLASSIFICATION`** (family `AgentSessionError`, Task 10), **`409 PROJECT_ARCHIVED`**, **`500 PROJECT_TEARDOWN_INCOMPLETE`** and **`409 PROJECT_LAUNCHED_NOT_DELETABLE`** (family `ProjectStateError`, Tasks 11–12). **A new family is four edits** — its class, its registry entries with `families: [<name>]`, its name in `api/error-codes.test.ts`'s `WIRE_CLASSES`, and its branch in `api/errors.ts` beside `SourceError`'s; it reuses `REQUEST_INVALID`, `SOURCE_SECRET_DETECTED`, `MEMBER_USER_NOT_FOUND`, `STEP_UP_REQUIRED`, `TOKEN_CREDENTIAL_REFUSED`, `TOKEN_PERSON_ONLY`, `CSRF_ORIGIN_REFUSED`, `SAML_LOGIN_NOT_BOUND` and the `AI_*` family. **Every new code is thrown as a literal** (`error-codes.test.ts` finds codes as `new SourceError('LITERAL'` and its siblings), and **every new code needs its meaning and remedy** in `api/error-codes.ts` (the reference's gate).
- **A REFUSAL TEST NEEDS A POSITIVE CONTROL IN THE SAME FILE**, and so does a negative claim. *"Refuses production output"* is true of a route that refuses every environment.
- **Never accept a check you have not watched fail.** Every task ends by breaking what it built, **after committing the task**, and naming the test that goes red with its assertion quoted. **Predict what turns red before you run it.** A control that stays green is a question to chase, not a result (ORIENTATION §9; the memory *a green control is a question*).
- **Every task names its CALLER.** A module with no call site is not built.
- **A MODEL KEY IS A CREDENTIAL.** It is in `startAgentSession`'s answer and nowhere else: never in a row, an event, an operator line, an error, an idempotency record, a test's snapshot or a log. **Unit tests mint against a recording fake LiteLLM** (Task 9's `fakeLiteLlm`); **Docker tests mint real keys through the running LiteLLM and end every session they start**, and their LiteLLM users are `mf-person-<a test user's id>`, which Task 9's orphan rule reclaims.
- **OUTPUT IS UNTRUSTED TEXT.** A line of an app's output is redacted before it leaves the control plane and is never written anywhere — not to a row, not to an operator line, not to an event (§14: *"read on request and never streamed"*).
- **A DESTRUCTIVE OPERATION IN A TEST TOUCHES ONLY WHAT THE TEST CREATED.** Archive and delete tests create their own project with their own slug prefix (`arch-`, `del-`), and **no test, probe or demo archives or deletes `launch-app`, `board-local`, `board-github`, or any project a demo relies on** — `demo-frontend` creates its own two. `destroyRepository` on driver 2 deletes a GitHub repository: **only ever against the fake** (the real conformance leg is Rich's, and this plan owes none).
- **Tests that run `git` set their own identity and ignore the machine's config**: `-c user.name=… -c user.email=… -c commit.gpgsign=false`, and `GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1` with `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL=/dev/null` wherever git reads configuration the test did not write.
- **Nothing in this plan installs an external package.** If a task believes it needs one, record a finding and raise it.
- **Ask before `sudo`.** No task needs it. **Never edit the spec** — Spec actions 1–4 go to Rich, and are applied only after he has read the words. **Never touch Laravel Valet.** These four containers must survive: `docker-simple-saml-saml-idp-1`, `qdrant-local-dev`, `mongodb`, `mongo-express`. **`caddy-data` must never be destroyed.** **`docker-simple-saml` is read-only** — Task 7's test users' `uid` goes in THIS repository's `infra/idp/config/authsources.php`.
- **macOS ships bash 3.2 and a BSD userland**: no associative arrays, no `mapfile`, no `xargs -r`, no `readlink -f`, `sed -i ''`. **The tool shell is zsh**: run multi-line probes with `bash <file>`, never name a loop variable `path`, and quote every variable holding several words (the authoring API's sitting 10, F20).
- **`request.log` writes nothing** under `Fastify({ logger: false })`. Use `console.error`, never with a secret, a key, a token, a cookie, an output line or a file's content in it. **A swallowed `.catch(() => undefined)` is this codebase's most productive defect** — and in a teardown, a swallowed failure is a live app its owner believes is off. **`.map(fn)` passes the ARRAY INDEX as its second argument.**
- **COMMIT ON `main`, AND STAGE YOUR OWN PATHS BY NAME**, meaning files, never directories. **Where a commit step says `<each path in Files, by name>`, it means exactly that**: every file the task's **Files** list names, plus a generated migration and its `drizzle/meta/` files, plus `openapi.json` and `schema.d.ts` when the contract moved. **Never `git add -A`, `git add .`, `git commit -a` or `git checkout .`.** Run `git status` before each commit and account for every path; other sessions commit on `main` while you work (a design session committed `e1712d8` the evening this plan was written). End every commit message with the attribution lines the session's own system reminder gives.
- **`$SCRATCH` IS YOUR SESSION'S SCRATCHPAD AND YOU MUST SET IT.**
  ```bash
  export SCRATCH=<your session's scratchpad directory>
  [ -d "$SCRATCH" ] || { echo 'SCRATCH is not a directory'; exit 1; }
  ```
- **Leave the machine as you found it.** `./scripts/snapshot-machine.sh` at the start and the end of a sitting, and `diff` the two. **At every close, run `bash scripts/dead-app-resources.sh`, `bash scripts/litellm-orphans.sh` and `bash scripts/app-images.sh` bare, then TRY each with `--apply` yourself**; hand the output to Rich only if you are refused. A sitting that started the GitHub fake and did not find it running stops it (`make github-down`) and says so. **A sitting that served anything on 7105 stops it.**
- **The control plane runs ONE source driver.** `make demo-frontend` runs on EITHER, like `make demo-authoring`; every other demo but those two and `make demo-github` is driver 1's, and each refuses the wrong driver before it creates anything (`require_driver`, `scripts/lib/api.sh`).

---

## Review Focus

**The five failure modes the spec implies and no task's happy path exercises, most likely first.** Each has its test added to the task that owns the code.

1. **An app's output carrying a secret — or production's being readable at all.** An app prints its own `MONGODB_URI`, a declared secret's value, or a token-shaped string, and an agent reads its output; or a client names a PRODUCTION instance's id. Expected: **every secret value `[REDACTED]`, exactly as in that instance's Incident; production refused `403 INSTANCE_OUTPUT_PRODUCTION` before the driver is asked.** Owned by Task 2's Docker case *redacts an app's own secrets from its recent output exactly as from its Incident* and Task 3's *refuses a production instance by its own code, and never asks the driver*.
2. **A model key that outlives what it was issued under** — the token that asked for it revoked, the project archived, its time run out, its cap or the person's month spent. Expected: **LiteLLM refuses the key in every case**, measured by calling it, not by reading a row. Owned by Task 10's Docker cases *a revoked token's sessions end, and LiteLLM refuses their keys* and *a session's key is refused past its cap*, and Task 11's *archiving ends every agent session, and LiteLLM refuses their keys*.
3. **Two origins crossing** — a sign-in started on `app` completing on the console, a session cookie from one origin used on the other, a page on one origin posting to the other's API. Expected: **each origin signs a person in on itself, and a mutation whose `Origin` is not the origin it arrived on is `403 CSRF_ORIGIN_REFUSED`**, whichever of the two it names. Owned by Task 8's *a sign-in begun on app completes on app, with app's cookie*, *refuses a mutation on app whose Origin is the console — and one on the console whose Origin is app*, and its Docker case *a real sign-in begun on the second origin completes on the second*.
4. **An archive interrupted half-way** — the control plane killed after the hostname moved and before the instances were retired, or a step failing. Expected: **the project reads archived, the hostname answers the switched-off page and never another app's, and the same request retried, or the next boot, finishes the teardown.** Owned by Task 11's *an archive interrupted after the route moved is finished by the next boot* and *a switched-off name answers 410 and never the wildcard*.
5. **Bytes smuggled past the text rules** — a script sent as base64, an executable with a PNG's extension, a key pasted into a PDF. Expected: **`400 REQUEST_INVALID` for text-as-bytes and for an unrecognised type; `409 SOURCE_SECRET_DETECTED` for the key, naming the path, never the value.** Owned by Task 4's *refuses text sent as bytes*, *refuses an executable whatever its name*, and *refuses a key inside a PDF's printable text*.

## The fixtures and helpers every snippet below uses

- **`withProjectServer(fn)`** (`api/testing.ts`): a fresh database, a server, `bio_prof` as owner of a project created **through the route**, a second project owned by `unrelated_user`, and `ctx.commitSha` — the seed's validated commit. **`sessionFor(ctx, puid, role?, { steppedUp })`**, **`mutationHeaders(deps)`**, **`refusal(res)`** and **`loginAs(deps, puid, { steppedUp })`** are beside it. **`testAiKeyService`** (`api/testing.ts:66-89`) mints nothing — Task 9 adds a recording fake beside it.
- **`describeSourceDriver(name, make, extra)`** (`source/driver-contract.ts`): the contract every source driver runs unchanged — driver 1 in `local-driver.test.ts`, driver 2 in `github/driver.test.ts` against the in-process fake. Its harness has `pushAsPerson(slug, files, message)` and `pushSymlinkAsPerson`.
- **`describeDriver`** (`runtime/driver-contract.ts`): the runtime contract, run by the fake in the unit tier and by the Docker driver in the Docker tier (`runtime/docker/driver.docker.test.ts`). **Read how it builds and starts an instance before writing a case**; Task 2's cases reuse its instance.
- **The Docker tier's deploy harness** — `releases/*.docker.test.ts` deploy a real app through `deployRelease` with a fixture blueprint; `releases/app-secrets.docker.test.ts` (the authoring API's Task 8) deploys **an app that prints a variable and exits 1**, which is the shape Task 2's redaction case extends. **Read it first.**
- **`SAMPLE_SECRETS`** (`build/testing.ts:16`): secret-shaped values built at run time, never a real one.
- **A token in a test** is minted through the route or written with `mintTestToken(db, { userId, projectId, capabilities, name, rateLimit })` as `api/authz-contract.ts` does; **check which of the two states your control is in** — a token written to the store can hold what no route would mint (ORIENTATION §3).
- **`ai/testing.ts`**: `litellmUrl()`, `litellmMasterKey()`, `ensureProbeUser`, `mintProbeKey`, `deleteProbeKey`, `deleteProbeKeyByAlias` — the Docker tier's direct line to the running LiteLLM.
- **`idp_login jar idp_jar login_url user pass expect_acs ca`** (`infra/lib/idp-login.sh`): a real sign-in through the Manifest IdP, **asserting the ACS the IdP posted to** — which is exactly the assertion Task 8's `app` sign-in needs.
- **`$SCRATCH`** as in *Global Constraints*.

## Task 2: One reader for an app's recent output — bounded per line and in all, stamped by Docker, shared with the Incident, redacted by the same rules

> **[M1] [M2] [M6] [M13] — MEASURED 2026-09-27 BY TASK 1 ([`spikes/frontend-baseline/`](../spikes/frontend-baseline/README.md)). Five corrections; the task's deliverable and sitting are unchanged.**
>
> 1. **Docker's `tail` counts `json-file` LOG ENTRIES of at most 16 KiB, not lines.** A 1 MiB line is 64 entries plus a 65th
>    holding its `\n`; `tail: 200` over a window holding one answered **136** lines and `tail: 1000` answered **936**. So Step
>    4's `tail: bounds.lines + 1` does NOT make `truncated.lines` known. Count the ENTRIES (frames) `demux` received; when they
>    equal the `tail` asked for, set `truncated.lines` and **drop the OLDEST line, which may be a fragment of a longer one** — a
>    tail can begin inside a long line (`tail: 2` began with `""`, the megabyte line's lone `\n`). `lines` is then *at most*,
>    and Task 14's guide says why. Whether to ask Docker for a larger tail and window it is this task's call; a read measured
>    25–33 ms and +15 MiB RSS with a megabyte line in the window.
> 2. **The stamp is per ENTRY, not per line.** Each 16 KiB chunk of a long line is its own frame carrying its own (identical)
>    `2026-09-27T09:02:57.196447669Z ` prefix, and the line's `\n` arrives as a 32-byte frame of stamp, space, `\n`. So
>    `stampOf` runs on EACH FRAME's payload before it joins `partial` — Step 2's parse *"on `\n`"* would leave 63 stamps
>    inside the megabyte line (past a 4 KiB cut, but counted in `cutBytes`, and in the text wherever `lineBytes` is absent).
>    Measured prefix: RFC 3339, UTC, nine fractional digits, one space — accept one to nine. **`Date.parse` reads nine digits**
>    to the millisecond, so Step 2's truncation before parsing is harmless rather than needed.
> 3. **An unterminated last line is not in Docker's log while the process runs.** It is written when the process EXITS, stamped
>    with the exit time (M1: `last line` never arrived in three reads; M2: it appeared after `docker stop`, stamped 27 s after it
>    was printed). **So Step 6's Docker case cannot hold as written for a running container** — *"exactly 50 lines, the megabyte
>    line cut with `cutBytes: 1_044_480`, … `last` present"*: `tail: 50` lands INSIDE the megabyte line's 65 entries, a stamped
>    long line's `cutBytes` counts its embedded stamps unless (2) is done, and `last` appears only after exit. Rewrite it from
>    these facts: a fixture whose long line is followed by more than `tail` short lines (the window starts clean); a second case
>    with the long line inside the window (its fragment dropped, `truncated.lines` true); `last` read after the container
>    exits. `demux`'s own partial flush stays right for what Docker delivers.
> 4. **The redaction case's set is the platform's own** — `deps.appSecrets.secretValues`, as Step 6 already says, and that
>    matters: the set holds `service:<name>:password`, never the URI (`services/credentials.ts:72`), so an app printing its
>    `MONGODB_URI` reads `mongodb://<user>:[REDACTED]@<host>:27017/<db>?authSource=admin` — the password redacted by its exact
>    match, the user and host kept. A set built from the URI alone leaves the password printed ALONE unredacted (measured). With
>    the real set, all fourteen of M6's lines matched their predictions; the residual is another app's 64-hex secret and an
>    8-letter password, both kept — Task 14's *What redaction does not catch*.
> 5. *(M13)* **18 hand-built lines in 13 files** (`grep -rn "stream: 'std\(out\|err\)'"` under `src/`, `docker/logs.ts` excluded);
